# Execution package

Generated by StackToIT from `execution-plan.json`. Nothing in this folder has been run: StackToIT reads and writes files and creates, changes and deletes nothing in any cloud. Every step is carried out by a person, by OpenTofu or by a script of this folder that a person runs. Paths are relative to the output folder.

Partially executable migration package · For review only · Not approved.

**What will be created.** OpenTofu creates 44 resource blocks; the scripts of this package make 5 STACKIT API calls. 16 source resources need no STACKIT resource. Architect work: 288 manual steps in 48 work packages.

**What can run as written.** 31 of the 49 automated steps (OpenTofu or a script) are free of decisions and manual prerequisites (1 waits for a manual prerequisite, 17 require a design decision) · 1 can run as written · 30 of those 31 wait for a value (project_id, carts_service_task_project_roles, catalog_service_task_project_roles, checkout_service_task_project_roles and 21 more); a person can start 120 of the 288 manual steps now (1 waits for a manual prerequisite, 5 wait for an automated step, 79 require a design decision, 83 are not executable as the plan stands); 2 steps of the plan are permanently unsupported (refused); 1 step of the plan has its verification incomplete: it carries critical settings of the source that no check validates, which a person confirms in the draft.

**Readiness levels.** 49 automated steps are in the draft · 31 are free of decisions and manual prerequisites · 1 rests on no default to confirm, no opt-in to answer and no value to supply · 0 are approved (every default and decision they rest on is confirmed in the decisions file, and no value they read is left to supply) · 30 of the steps free of decisions and manual prerequisites wait for a value (project_id, carts_service_task_project_roles, catalog_service_task_project_roles, checkout_service_task_project_roles and 21 more).

**What the figures count.** Three measures, not one: "can run as written" means the step is free of decisions and manual prerequisites and waits for no value (1 of the 49 automated steps); "automation-eligible" means the critical settings of the unit are closed (0 of 44 units; 29 more become eligible once their open items are answered; 15 cannot be automated in this build: a target resource the provider marks experimental (10 units) and 4 more reasons, every one on the Automation eligibility line of index.html); "waiting for a value" means the step is free of decisions and manual prerequisites and reads a variable that has no value yet, so a person supplies it before the step runs and the step is never approved before (30 of the 31 steps free of decisions and manual prerequisites: the plan lists 41 OpenTofu variables, the STACKIT project id among them, with no value).

**What cannot be preserved.** 309 critical settings of 50 source resources that the draft does not carry are open as manual or refused steps ([engineering/not-carried.csv](../engineering/not-carried.csv) lists what the draft does not carry, property by property). 2 steps of the plan are refused: the target has no equivalent (see What cannot be migrated).

**Decisions required.** 10 decisions (22 questions) must be answered before the steps that wait for them can be carried out ([Required decisions](#required-decisions); described in [executive/decisions.md](../executive/decisions.md)). These are the decisions that steps of the plan wait for; the decisions to make of the register (decisions.json) also count the manual actions and plan statements that need a person, so that number can be larger.

**First work package.** [`WP-02`](manual/actions.md), Set up the identities and access (prepare) — owner: Identity Architect, 5 manual steps. The next package that waits, `WP-08` (Write the security group rules the draft does not write (security)), waits for decisions (10 of 13 manual steps). It opens when `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_security_group.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_security_group.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.checkout_service.aws_security_group.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.orders_service.aws_security_group.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.ui_service.aws_security_group.this` are answered.

## How the package is carried out

This is an operator-executable migration plan: a person, OpenTofu or a script that a person runs carries out each step, in the order below, and nothing in this folder runs by itself. OpenTofu applies the OpenTofu steps together in one `tofu apply`, so the manual steps and STACKIT API calls that come before it are not enforced: `execution/terraform/apply.md` lists them as a checklist to complete first. `execution/deploy.sh` is the one entry point that carries the steps out in that order for you, phase by phase up to the verification: with no argument it prints the order and changes nothing, `--run` carries the steps out and stops at each step that waits for a decision, a value, an approval or a person, and running it again resumes from `review/progress.yaml`. StackToIT has not run it. An executor inside StackToIT that would carry the plan out step by step (`stacktoit execute`) is designed and not implemented.

## Verdict

**Partially executable migration package**

Steps: partially executable: some steps cannot be carried out as the plan stands, whatever is decided: they are marked not executable under Execution order.

The verdict is of the 339 steps of the plan of this package. Every resource and data block of the draft (44) is a step of it. Every source resource of the assessment (52) is a step of it, or needs neither a STACKIT resource nor a step (1). Of them, 16 need no STACKIT resource (`terraform/README.md` lists them under Not needed on STACKIT); 15 of them still have a step of the plan. Not needed on STACKIT, and why: `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_internet_gateway.this` (No internet-gateway object: a routed STACKIT network reaches the internet with no route and no routing table (observed on the target in a project outside a Network Area, tested on 2026-10-10); inbound access is decided by public IPs and security groups). These files of the draft hold no step of the plan at all: `terraform/terraform.tf`, `terraform/variables.tf`.

Execution completeness: COMPLETE (each of the 288 manual steps states what it is, why, who carries it out and when it is done, and the input it needs where it depends on one).

31 of the 49 automated steps (OpenTofu or a script) are free of decisions and manual prerequisites (1 waits for a manual prerequisite, 17 require a design decision) · 1 can run as written · 30 of those 31 wait for a value (project_id, carts_service_task_project_roles, catalog_service_task_project_roles, checkout_service_task_project_roles and 21 more); a person can start 120 of the 288 manual steps now (1 waits for a manual prerequisite, 5 wait for an automated step, 79 require a design decision, 83 are not executable as the plan stands); 2 steps of the plan are permanently unsupported (refused); 1 step of the plan has its verification incomplete: it carries critical settings of the source that no check validates, which a person confirms in the draft. Migration-ready: NO — 309 critical settings of 50 source resources have manual or refused steps open (307 manual, 2 refused); 181 steps of the plan cannot be carried out now (96 wait only for a decision, 83 are not executable whatever is decided, 2 are refused).

The critical settings of the source that the draft does not carry, by source resource: each is a manual or refused step under Execution order (a source resource the draft writes no resource for carries them in its own step); `assurance/critical-semantics.json` says why each is open and what closes it. A setting the knowledge base names by a generated id is said in words before its id: the source attribute it is, as the step of the setting states it, or else its id in words where the plan states no attribute for it.

## Why some units are not carried by Terraform or the STACKIT API

51 units that Terraform and the STACKIT API do not carry are counted here once each, under one reason. Whatever else is open on a unit is in `execution-plan.json`.

- 7 units: STACKIT has a counterpart, but it does not do something these units rely on. `module.dependencies.module.catalog_opensearch.aws_opensearch_domain.this`, `module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_security_group.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_security_group.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.checkout_service.aws_security_group.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.orders_service.aws_security_group.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.ui_service.aws_security_group.this`
- 2 units: they need a different design on STACKIT, which an architect has to decide. `module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_event_rule.ecs_events`
- 16 units: a decision of yours is open. `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`, `module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default`, `module.dependencies.module.orders_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_db`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_opensearch`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.mq`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.orders_db` and 8 more
- 25 units: the assessment is not finished: a setting is still open for review, which says nothing yet about whether STACKIT can carry them. `module.dependencies.aws_security_group.catalog_opensearch_sg`, `module.dependencies.aws_security_group.mq`, `module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_security_group.this`, `module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.module.aws_security_group.aws_security_group.cbd`, `module.dependencies.module.orders_rds.aws_security_group.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_container_insights`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_resource_policy.eventbridge_ecs`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_kms_key.cmk` and 17 more
- 1 unit: a person carries it out by hand. `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_ecs_cluster.cluster`

## Solution options

Where a capability can be met in more than one way, the ways are listed together, in the order of the plan. The order says nothing about which is better. Choosing one is a decision of yours; nothing here has been chosen for you.

### Event-pattern rule: routes the events that match a pattern to its targets

For: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_event_rule.ecs_events`

- Knative Eventing (release 1.24.0): a solution the customer runs
  - Carries part of what the listed units use.
  - Carries: event pattern routing.
  - Does not carry: A pattern that looks inside the body of an event, not only at its attributes.
  - Does not carry (critical): The events that a service of the source cloud emits about itself (a change in a storage, a database or an audit log). The service that emits them does not exist on STACKIT, so a rule that matches them has no event to match there.
  - Needs: a stackit_ske_cluster on STACKIT.
  - Price: not known (UNPRICED), which is not a price of zero.
  - To leave it: The project does not state this; it follows from what the product is. Triggers and subscriptions are declarative Kubernetes objects, the payload is CloudEvents over HTTP, and events not yet delivered persist in the backing Kafka or RabbitMQ.
  - Ownership afterwards: The customer runs and owns the installation and everything in it, and there is no operator to hand anything over.
  - Can be chosen for this target.
  - It has not been run on STACKIT; what is said of it rests on documentation.

## What cannot be migrated

- the critical setting `credentials-and-keys-settings` of `module.dependencies.module.catalog_opensearch.aws_opensearch_domain.this`: the target has no counterpart that carries the setting `credentials-and-keys-settings` of aws_opensearch_domain (the knowledge base marks it UNSUPPORTED). The mapping rule for this kind of unit found a setting the target has no counterpart for. The IAM master user, the master user name and password, the customer KMS key and the custom endpoint certificate, read where they are set whatever their value. STACKIT generates the OpenSearch credential (stackit_opensearch_credential username and password are computed), offers no customer KMS key for the instance and no custom endpoint certificate (pinned provider); a password is never carried. Reason: the target has no counterpart that carries this setting of the source (`TARGET_SETTING_UNSUPPORTED`).
- the critical setting `domain-credentials-and-keys` of `module.dependencies.module.catalog_opensearch.aws_opensearch_domain.this`: the target has no counterpart that carries the setting `domain-credentials-and-keys` of aws_opensearch_domain (the knowledge base marks it UNSUPPORTED). The mapping rule for this kind of unit found a setting the target has no counterpart for. STACKIT generates the OpenSearch credential (stackit_opensearch_credential username and password are computed), offers no customer KMS key for the instance and no custom endpoint certificate (pinned provider). Reason: the target has no counterpart that carries this setting of the source (`TARGET_SETTING_UNSUPPORTED`).

The draft writes no resource for 7 source resources: a person decides how each moves to STACKIT and sets its target up by hand (Manual steps):

- `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_iam_role.task_role` — a plan-time input of the source switches off module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_iam_role_policy_attachment.cloudwatch_agent, part of module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_iam_role.task_role: the draft writes none of it and none of its settings was assessed.
- `module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_iam_role.task_role` — a plan-time input of the source switches off module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_iam_role_policy_attachment.cloudwatch_agent, part of module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_iam_role.task_role: the draft writes none of it and none of its settings was assessed.
- `module.retail_app_ecs.module.checkout_service.aws_iam_role.task_role` — a plan-time input of the source switches off module.retail_app_ecs.module.checkout_service.aws_iam_role_policy_attachment.cloudwatch_agent, part of module.retail_app_ecs.module.checkout_service.aws_iam_role.task_role: the draft writes none of it and none of its settings was assessed.
- `module.retail_app_ecs.module.orders_service.aws_iam_role.task_role` — a plan-time input of the source switches off module.retail_app_ecs.module.orders_service.aws_iam_role_policy_attachment.cloudwatch_agent, part of module.retail_app_ecs.module.orders_service.aws_iam_role.task_role: the draft writes none of it and none of its settings was assessed.

- `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_event_rule.ecs_events` — Decided when OpenTofu plans: its count is `(var.container_insights_setting == "enhanced" && var.lifecycle_events_enabled) ? 1 : 0`, which reads 0 as the variables stand, so the draft writes no resource for it. It is not dropped: assess again with a --var-file that sets the input, or decide it after a plan. Event-pattern rules react to AWS service events that do not exist on STACKIT.
- `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_container_insights` — Decided when OpenTofu plans: its count is `(var.container_insights_setting == "enhanced" && var.lifecycle_events_enabled) ? 1 : 0`, which reads 0 as the variables stand, so the draft writes no resource for it. It is not dropped: assess again with a --var-file that sets the input, or decide it after a plan. the source creates no instance of the log source (count or for_each yields none, or it is switched off): no stackit_logs_instance is drafted.

83 steps of the plan are not executable whatever is decided; each says why under Execution order. In all, 181 steps of the plan cannot be carried out now (96 wait only for a decision, 83 are not executable whatever is decided, 2 are refused).

- `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-API-OPT-IN` — Allow calls of the Secrets Manager Vault-compatible API, whose maturity is not established, for this migration (the API may change or vanish)? To answer: write a decisions file (YAML or JSON, at most 1 MiB) with `schema_version` `0.2.0` and the head of `decisions.json` (`source_digest`, `kb_version` and `kb_sha256`), and under `decisions` copy the entry of each record you answer from the `template` of `decisions.json`. Fill in `resolution` (`RESOLVED_BY_ARCHITECT`, `ACCEPTED_DIFFERENCE`, `ACCEPTED_LOSS`, `ACCEPTED_REDESIGN`, or `REJECTED` when the proposal is not accepted: the decision then stays open), `reviewed_by` (the role that answers), `reviewed_at` (the date), `rationale`, `evidence_refs` (at least one reference for an answer that accepts) and, for a record that states options, `chosen_option`; keep `id`, `members`, `members_sha256` and `artifact_sha256` as the template gives them. Then run `stacktoit assess --decisions FILE` on the same source: an answer frees only the steps its effect allows, and a freed step says which answer freed it.

## Required credentials

- OpenTofu: the STACKIT provider reads its credentials from the environment, for example `STACKIT_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY_PATH` (the path of a service account key file); the account needs the roles that create the resources of the steps. `terraform/README.md` says what the draft needs.
- OpenTofu variables a step needs a value for before it runs (a variable without a default, or one the plan does not take as given): `alb_cluster_network_id`, `alb_health_ok_status`, `alb_interval_jitter`, `alb_target_ips`, `carts_service_task_project_roles`, `catalog_opensearch_plan_name`, `catalog_opensearch_version`, `catalog_rds_plan_name`, `catalog_service_task_project_roles`, `checkout_service_task_project_roles`, `cluster_api_allowed_cidrs`, `cluster_machine_type`, `cluster_nodes_max`, `cluster_nodes_min`, `dynamodb_tables_allowed_cidrs`, `dynamodb_tables_cpu`, `dynamodb_tables_ram`, `dynamodb_tables_storage_gb`, `elasticache_redis_allowed_cidrs`, `elasticache_redis_version`, `eventbridge_ecs_count`, `eventbridge_ecs_logs_instance_id`, `kms_access_scope`, `kms_protection`, `logs_acl_retention_180d`, `mongodbflex_storage_class`, `mq_version`, `orders_rds_storage_size`, `orders_service_task_project_roles`, `postgresflex_storage_class`, `private_count`, `private_ipv4_prefixes`, `project_id`, `public_count`, `public_ipv4_prefixes`, `sg_carts_service_name`, `sg_catalog_service_name`, `sg_checkout_service_name`, `sg_orders_service_name`, `sg_ui_service_name`, `ui_service_task_project_roles`.
- OpenTofu variables the steps read with the default the draft declares (check each): `alb_labels`, `alb_plan_id`, `availability_zones`, `catalog_opensearch_allowed_cidrs`, `catalog_rds_allowed_cidrs`, `catalog_rds_version`, `default_ipv4_nameservers`, `dynamodb_tables_pitr_hours`, `dynamodb_tables_version`, `elasticache_redis_plan_name`, `mq_allowed_cidrs`, `mq_plan_name`, `orders_rds_access_scope`, `orders_rds_admin_roles`, `orders_rds_allowed_cidrs`, `region`, `sg_alb_labels`, `sg_alb_sg_labels`.
- API scripts: each reads its credentials and inputs from the environment when it runs, and stops when one is not set: `STACKIT_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_DOCUMENT_CATALOG_DB`, `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_DOCUMENT_CATALOG_OPENSEARCH`, `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_DOCUMENT_MQ`, `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_DOCUMENT_ORDERS_DB`, `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_NAME_CATALOG_DB`, `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_NAME_CATALOG_OPENSEARCH`, `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_NAME_MQ`, `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_NAME_ORDERS_DB`, `TF_VAR_project_id`, `TF_VAR_region`. No script holds a secret. `STACKIT_INPUT_INSTANCEID`, `STACKIT_INPUT_PASSWORD`, `STACKIT_INPUT_USERNAME`: the override of a value the draft writes as an output; when it is not set and tofu is installed, the script reads it with `tofu -chdir=terraform output -raw <output>` once the draft is applied (the request file lists the output of each). `STACKIT_API_URL_SECRETS_MANAGER_VAULT`, `STACKIT_API_URL_SERVICE_ENABLEMENT` may name another base address; without it the script uses the documented address of the service for the region of the draft.

## Execution order

The steps in the order they are carried out: a step comes after every step it depends on. A step is named here without the name of the generator that made it; `execution-plan.json` holds the full id of each.

A text that many steps share word for word is written once here, with what is the step's own in braces (`{name}` for the name of a resource, `{address}` for an IP address or network, `{steps}` for the steps a state names). A line below names the wording of what its step does (W) and the state it is in (S), and gives after "Where" what stands for the braces.

- **S1** (39 steps of the plan): not executable as the plan stands: StackToIT performs none of this: it deletes nothing in any cloud, and no step of this plan automates the retirement; it also waits for 18 decisions: `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-API-OPT-IN` and 17 more (decision group `BS-eb9126787384` of execution-plan.json)

The OpenTofu step for stackit_valkey_instance.dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default (`TF/stackit_valkey_instance.dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default`) — OpenTofu: creates `stackit_valkey_instance.dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default` (`terraform/data.tf`). (not executable — requires a design decision — blocked by `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default`; and, as the plan states: the decision DEC-GEN-CONVERT-SIZE-FROM-SOURCE is open and this step stands on its DEFAULT, option use-the-size-derived-from-the-source: StackToIT carried out the option use-the-size-derived-from-the-source for module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default by default: var.elasticache_redis_plan_name defaults to stackit-keyvalue-1.4.10-single (node type cache.t3.micro has 2 vCPU and 0.5 GiB (AWS price list), and the source is a single node: the smallest single-node Key Value Store plan of the eu01 catalogue (2026-10-18) that holds 0.5 GiB (0.5369 GB at 10^9 bytes per GB) of memory (the plan table gives its RAM as "GB" and does not say whether a GB is 10^9 or 2^30 bytes, so the plan is chosen to hold the source's memory when a GB is 10^9 bytes, which it then also does when a GB is 2^30 bytes (it is never smaller than the source whichever way the table is read)): 1 vCPU / 4 GB RAM / 10 GB disk per node; the disk is not a figure of the source, so the smallest disk of that size is taken and the disk is open). The target instance must be empty before the load (the STACKIT how-to of the restore says so): load the dump with the mysql client, as the user of the instance's service key, into the empty instance: `mysql --host=\<target-host> --port=\<target-port> --user=\<target-user> --password --database=\<target-database> < \<dump-file>.sql 2> load.log`, and read every line of `load.log`. A DEFINER account that does not exist on the target, a collation the target does not know and a database the target does not have are the errors the checks name: each is settled by a person, never by skipping the object. Read the depth in CloudWatch: Amazon MQ for RabbitMQ publishes MessageCount (the sum of the ready and the unacknowledged messages) for the broker and, on 3.x brokers, for each queue (the dimensions Queue and VirtualHost; on 4.x brokers the figures of a queue come from the Prometheus endpoint), and Amazon MQ for ActiveMQ publishes QueueSize and InFlightCount for each queue and TotalMessageCount for the broker. On a RabbitMQ broker the management API gives the same figure for every queue: `curl -s -u "\<source-user>:$SOURCE_PASSWORD" "\<source-broker-endpoint>/api/queues" | jq '[.[] | (.messages_ready // 0) + (.messages_unacknowledged // 0)] | add'` is 0 when they are empty. An inactive durable topic subscriber (InactiveDurableTopicSubscribersCount) holds messages that only its own client can read: connect it to the AWS broker until it has read them. ## Terraform commands

`execution/terraform/apply.md` lists every OpenTofu step with its resource and file. OpenTofu applies the whole draft in one run and enforces none of the manual steps, STACKIT API calls or phases of the plan: complete the steps that `execution/terraform/apply.md` lists under "Complete these steps before tofu apply" first. 13 OpenTofu steps are not executable yet: a full apply creates every block of the draft as it stands, theirs included, so apply only when every OpenTofu step is executable. From the output folder:

```sh
cd terraform
tofu init
tofu validate
tofu plan -out=stackit.tfplan
tofu apply stackit.tfplan
```

## API commands

Each call has a request file (the call as data) and a script that renders it. A script is run with `bash <script>` from any folder; it stops before the call when its step is not executable, when an input is not set, or when the call needs a confirmation.

- step 1: `execution/api/service-enablement/001-enableserviceregional.sh` (executable: it can run as written)
- step 75: `execution/api/secrets-manager-vault/001-writesecret.sh` (not executable — requires a design decision — blocked by `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-API-OPT-IN`; and, as the plan states: Required input: STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_DOCUMENT_CATALOG_OPENSEARCH names a file (keep it outside the package and out of version control) that holds the JSON object of the key/value pairs the secret at the path STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_NAME_CATALOG_OPENSEARCH names has on STACKIT. StackToIT never reads the value of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_opensearch from the source, so the document is yours: the same value from a secure source, or a new one. The source builds the value (its version resource, attribute secret_string) from a value it generates itself (module.dependencies.random_string.catalog_opensearch_master): the document holds the matching values on STACKIT (for a generated value, one you generate or choose for STACKIT: the generated one is only in the source's state), under the keys the consumers of the secret read (the key names are in the source's secret_string, which the scan does not read); To make the document from AWS, if the value is still there, run this yourself with your own AWS credentials (StackToIT never runs it): `aws secretsmanager get-secret-value --secret-id \<the AWS secret id> --query SecretString --output text` into the file STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_DOCUMENT_CATALOG_OPENSEARCH names (a value of key/value pairs is a JSON object already), and delete that file after the write; The path of the secret is an input: STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_NAME_CATALOG_OPENSEARCH names it (the source does not state the name as a literal the plan can rely on), so this step does not carry the setting `name` and its manual step stays; the applications read the secret by the path you choose; The step logs in as the migration user stackit_secretsmanager_user.migration (write_enabled = true), which reaches every secret of stackit_secretsmanager_instance.migrated_secrets and whose generated password is in the OpenTofu state (a sensitive attribute): delete the user (remove its block from the draft and apply) once the secrets are written. The execution order names, as "Step state", how far the step of a call has come: "generated, not yet tested offline" (a converter wrote it and no test ran its script against a local stub), "generated, tested offline" (a test of StackToIT ran the script under a real shell against a local stub), "verified live" (a recorded probe against the STACKIT API shows the step does what it states) and "catalogued, not generated" (the call is known and described and no converter writes it). StackToIT runs none of these calls itself.

A script compares the answer of its call with the answer the plan expects (the HTTP status exactly, and a member when the plan names one). It stops with exit 3 when its step is not executable, 4 when the call needs the confirmation, 5 when an input, a tool or a file is missing or unsafe, 6 when its lookup cannot tell whether the object exists, 7 when the answer is not the expected one, and 8 when a postcondition it checks does not hold.

A script that calls a STACKIT API with curl trusts the certificate authorities curl trusts. For an API behind a private authority, set `STACKIT_CA_BUNDLE` to a PEM file of its certificates (curl's --cacert); the certificate is always verified. Each call ends after `STACKIT_CALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` seconds (120 unless it names others, at most 3600), so an API that does not answer stops the script with a non-zero exit instead of holding it; a call that timed out may still have been made, so read the target before running the script again.

A script that writes a secret into STACKIT Secrets Manager (the Vault-compatible API) logs in as the Secrets Manager user the draft creates, with the username and password that user has once the draft is applied (the script reads them from the draft's outputs with tofu output, or from its `STACKIT_INPUT_USERNAME` and `STACKIT_INPUT_PASSWORD` variables), and writes the document of key/value pairs that the file named in its `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_DOCUMENT_<n>` variable holds. The document is a file of yours: the package holds no value of any secret, and the script prints no value, puts none on a command line and keeps none outside its own temporary folder. A secret that already has a current version stops the script (exit 4) until `STACKIT_CONFIRM_REPLACE` is set to the path of the secret, because the write makes a new version the current one. The user is a migration user with write access to every secret of the instance and its password is in the OpenTofu state: delete the user (remove its block from the draft and apply) once the secrets are written.

## Manual steps

288 steps are carried out by a person, each described in `execution/manual/actions.md`: what, why, who, when, what it needs and when it is done. Roles named: Data Architect, Identity Architect, Migration Owner, Network Architect, Platform Architect, Security Architect, Storage Architect.

## Verification

`execution/verify/checks.md` lists 295 checks to hold before the steps and 62 checks to hold after them, one checklist row each. 1 of them confirms a critical setting the draft carries (verification only): a person confirms it in the file of the draft, and no step sets it up by hand.

## Rollback

Undoing a step: 60 can be undone (the step says how), 10 only in part (what was written since is lost), 264 by a person, 5 cannot be undone. From the cutover on, steps 210, 211, 212, 213, 214, 215, 216, 217, 218, 219, 220, 221, 222, 223, 224, 225, 226, 227, 228, 229, 230, 231, 232, 233, 234, 235, 236, 237, 238, 239, 240, 241, 243, 244, 245, 246, 247, 248, 249, 250, 251, 252, 253, 254, 255, 256, 257, 258, 259, 260, 261, 262, 263, 264, 265, 282, 283, 284, 285, 286, 287, 288, 289, 290, 291, 292, 293, 294, 295, 296, 297, 298, 299, 300, 301, 302, 303, 304, 305, 306, 307, 308, 309, 310, 311, 312, 313, 314, 315, 316, 317, 318, 319, 320, 321, 322, 323, 324, 325, 326, 327, 328, 329, 330, 331, 332, 333, 334, 335, 336, 337, 338, 339 cannot be undone in full by a tool: decide how before the cutover starts. Each step's rollback is in the actions sheet and, for a manual step, in `execution/manual/actions.md`.

## Files of this package

- `execution-plan.json` — the plan itself, machine-readable, at the root of the run: the one file of the plan (the package holds no copy of it)
- `execution/terraform/apply.md` — the OpenTofu steps and commands
- `execution/api/` — one request file and one script for each STACKIT API call
- `execution/manual/actions.md` — the manual steps and what is not migrated
- `execution/verify/checks.md` — the checks before and after each step
- `migration/execution-actions.csv` — one row per step, for a spreadsheet
- `migration/work-packages.csv` — one row per work package of the manual steps, for a spreadsheet
